Method: Combined multi-probe experimental approach: terahertz time-domain spectroscopy to probe low-energy magnetic excitations as a function of field direction and strength, complementary Raman spectroscopy to resolve magnon, spinon, and phonon excitations, and high-field magnetization measurements to independently identify spin-flop transitions.

Result: Magnetic excitation spectra show pronounced field-direction dependence. For B∥a and B∥c*, spectra undergo abrupt reconstructions at spin-flop transitions. For B∥b, no spin-flop occurs; instead the spectrum evolves continuously with strong terahertz polarization dependence as the ferromagnetic subsystem progressively polarizes. At higher fields, the complex low-field spectrum collapses to a reduced set of broad excitations indicating weakened inter-chain coupling. Raman spectroscopy resolves magnon/spinon modes and their temperature- and field-evolution.

Conclusion: A magnetic field reorganizes the coupled ferromagnetic and antiferromagnetic chain subsystems in Cu₂(OH)₃Br in a strongly direction-dependent manner, mapping out field-induced phase transitions where spin-flop physics dominates in certain orientations while progressive ferromagnetic polarization governs others, ultimately decoupling the subsystems at high fields.

Abstract: We present magnetic-field-dependent evolution of magnetization and spin dynamics in the quasi-two-dimensional spin-$1/2$ magnet $\mathrm{Cu_2(OH)_3Br}$, consisting of alternately coupled ferromagnetic Cu1 and antiferromagnetic Cu2 spin chains. Terahertz spectroscopy reveals a pronounced field-direction dependence of the low-energy magnetic excitation spectrum. For magnetic fields applied perpendicular to the spin chains, $B\parallel a$ and $B\parallel c^*$, the spectra undergo abrupt reconstructions at the spin-flop transitions identified independently by high-field magnetization measurements. For $B\parallel b$, by contrast, no spin-flop occurs; instead, the excitation spectrum evolves continuously with field and exhibits a strong terahertz radiation polarization dependence as the ferromagnetic Cu1 subsystem becomes progressively polarized. At higher fields, the complex low-field spectrum is replaced by a reduced set of broad excitations, consistent with a weakening of the coupling between the ferromagnetic and antiferromagnetic chain subsystems. Complementary Raman spectroscopy resolves magnon and spinon excitations alongside several phonon modes and traces the characteristic temperature- and magnetic-field-dependent evolution of the magnetic excitations. The combined spectroscopic and magnetization results map out how a magnetic field reorganizes the coupled ferromagnetic and antiferromagnetic subsystems in $\mathrm{Cu_2(OH)_3Br}$ across field-induced phase transitions.

</details>


### [167] [Signatures of spin-wave dynamics in quasiparticle interference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06398)
*Alireza Akbari,Peter Thalmeier*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper theoretically investigates momentum-resolved quasiparticle interference (QPI) in a localized-itinerant antiferromagnet with exchange-coupled local moments and conduction electrons. It finds a characteristic crossover in the QPI spectrum at the lower magnon-emission edge: below this scale the self-energy's real part dominates (dispersive modifications), while above it the imaginary part causes broadening and redistribution. The dynamical self-energy effects persist beyond the upper magnon energy and are strongly bias-asymmetric for a particle-hole-asymmetric band, showing that FT tunneling spectroscopy can distinguish static magnetic reconstruction from dynamical spin-wave renormalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish static antiferromagnetic band reconstruction from dynamical spin-wave renormalization effects using momentum-resolved quasiparticle interference as a spectroscopic probe.

Method: The authors incorporate a momentum- and frequency-dependent self-energy arising from one-magnon processes directly into the Born impurity scattering formalism to compute the QPI spectrum in a localized-itinerant antiferromagnet.

Result: A characteristic crossover in QPI at the lower magnon emission edge: dispersive modification governed by the real part of the self-energy below it, and pronounced broadening/redistribution from the imaginary part above it. Dynamical response persists beyond the upper magnon energy and shows strong tunneling-bias asymmetry for a particle-hole-asymmetric band.

Conclusion: Fourier-transform tunneling spectroscopy can detect and distinguish static magnetic reconstruction (band folding) from dynamical spin-wave renormalization via the distinct fingerprints in the momentum-resolved QPI spectrum.

Abstract: We investigate momentum-resolved quasiparticle interference (QPI) in a localized-itinerant antiferromagnet, where ordered local moments are exchange coupled to conduction electrons. Static antiferromagnetic order reconstructs the electronic bands, while one-magnon processes further dress the quasiparticles through a momentum- and frequency-dependent self-energy. Incorporating this dynamical renormalization directly into the Born impurity scattering, we find a characteristic crossover in the QPI spectrum. Below the lower magnon-emission edge, the modification is predominantly dispersive and governed by the real part of the self-energy, whereas above this scale its imaginary part produces pronounced broadening and redistribution of the scattering intensity. The dynamical response persists beyond the upper magnon energy and is strongly asymmetric in tunneling bias for a particle--hole-asymmetric band. These results show that Fourier-transform tunneling spectroscopy can distinguish static magnetic reconstruction from dynamical spin-wave renormalization.

</details>


### [168] [NMR evidence of pressure-induced structural transition and enhanced spin fluctuations up to 14~GPa in SrCu$_2$(BO$_3$)$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06472)
*Zhanlong Wu,Kefan Du,Shuo Li,Tong Shi,Ying Chen,Qingxin Dong,Rui Zhou,Rong Yu,Juanjuan Liu,Bosen Wang,Jinguang Cheng,Weiqiang Yu,Yi Cui*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: High-pressure ¹¹B NMR measurements on SrCu₂(BO₃)₂ up to 14 GPa reveal two pressure-induced monoclinic phases and map out a temperature-pressure phase diagram containing a gapless 3D antiferromagnetic phase (above 4 GPa, below 10 K), a 2D short-range ordered antiferromagnetic phase (~20 K), and an extended correlated paramagnetic regime (above 30 K), resolving controversies about pressure-induced phase transitions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Shastry-Sutherland compound SrCu₂(BO₃)₂ is a key platform for studying quantum phases and quantum phase transitions driven by magnetic frustration, but the nature of its pressure-induced structural and magnetic phase transitions remains controversial.

Method: Performed high-pressure ¹¹B nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) measurements up to 14 GPa, analyzing NMR spectra and spin-lattice relaxation rate (1/T₁) as functions of pressure and temperature.

Result: NMR spectra show two pressure-induced monoclinic phases. Above 4 GPa and below 10 K, rapid spectral broadening and power-law 1/T₁ behavior indicate a gapless 3D antiferromagnetic (AFM) phase. Around 20 K, field-dependent NMR line splits reveal a 2D short-range ordered AFM phase. Above 30 K, sublinear power-law 1/T₁ behavior indicates an extended correlated paramagnetic regime.

Conclusion: The study provides clear NMR evidence for a gapless 3D AFM phase in the monoclinic phase and resolves a multi-regime phase diagram (3D AFM, 2D short-range ordered AFM, and correlated paramagnet) for SrCu₂(BO₃)₂ under pressure, clarifying the controversial pressure-induced phase behavior.

Abstract: The Shastry-Sutherland compound SrCu$_2$(BO$_3$)$_2$ has attracted considerable interest as a platform for exploring quantum phases and quantum phase transitions driven by magnetic frustration. The pressure-induced structural and magnetic phase transitions in SrCu$_2$(BO$_3$)$_2$, however, remain controversial. To address this issue, we performed high-pressure $^{11}$B nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) measurements on SrCu$_2$(BO$_3$)$_2$ up to 14~GPa. The NMR spectra reveal two pressure-induced monoclinic phases. With pressure above 4~GPa and with temperature below 10~K, the rapid broadening of the NMR spectrum and the power-law behavior in the spin-lattice relaxation rate $1/T_1$ provide clear evidence for a gapless 3D antiferromagnetic (AFM) phase in the monoclinic phase. At an intermediate temperature range around 20~K, the emergence of the field-dependent NMR line splits resolves a two-dimensional, short-range ordered AFM phase; at temperature above 30~K, the sublinear power-law behavior of $1/T_1$ identifies an extended correlated paramagnetic regime.

</details>


### [169] [Electronic correlations and fluctuating lattice distortions in vanadium dioxide](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06707)
*Antonio Picano,Martin Eckstein,Francesco Grandi*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper develops a stochastic semiclassical extension of dynamical mean-field theory that couples correlated electrons with fluctuating lattice distortions via non-conservative Langevin dynamics, extending prior work to nonlinear electron-phonon coupling and multiple lattice modes. Applied to a two-orbital model of vanadium dioxide, it finds two lattice distortions melt at separated temperatures, yielding insulating, intermediate metallic, and high-symmetry phases with distinct microscopic origins.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Structural phase transitions accompanying metal-insulator transitions are typically described within coherent lattice approximations where lattice coordinates are fixed by energy minimization, ignoring lattice entropy. However, the partition of transition entropy between electrons and lattice often determines the transition temperature. A framework is needed that treats both correlated electrons and fluctuating lattice degrees of freedom on equal footing.

Method: The authors develop a stochastic semiclassical extension of dynamical mean-field theory in which correlated electrons and fluctuating lattice distortions evolve together. Key extensions over previous work include (1) incorporating nonlinear electron-phonon interactions and (2) coupling several lattice modes simultaneously. The resulting non-conservative Langevin equations feature deterministic forces, damping, and correlated noise generated self-consistently by the interacting electron subsystem. The electronic part is treated within nonequilibrium dynamical mean-field theory.

Result: Applied to a minimal two-orbital model of VO2's monoclinic phase with two symmetry-distinct distortions, the approach shows the two distortions melt at well-separated temperatures, producing three phases: insulating, intermediate metallic, and high-symmetry. The dimerization melting is driven by electron correlation coupling and is already captured by the coherent lattice approximation, while the high-symmetry phase restoration requires the entropy of the fluctuating lattice.

Conclusion: The framework captures lattice-entropy-driven structural transitions that coherent lattice approximations miss, successfully distinguishing different microscopic origins of successive transitions in VO2. Since the electronic subsystem uses nonequilibrium DMFT, the approach can be extended to photoexcited systems with nonthermal electronic distributions in future work.

Abstract: Metal-insulator transitions in correlated materials are often accompanied by a change of crystal structure. They are commonly described within a coherent lattice approximation, which combines a correlated treatment of the electrons with a lattice represented by one or a few classical distortion coordinates fixed by minimizing an energy. The structural degrees of freedom then carry no entropy of their own, even though the partition of the transition entropy between electrons and lattice is often what decides the transition temperature. Here we develop a stochastic semiclassical extension of dynamical mean-field theory in which correlated electrons and fluctuating lattice distortions are evolved together, extending previous formulations from linear to nonlinear electron-phonon interactions and from a single to several coupled lattice modes. The resulting non-conservative Langevin equations have deterministic forces, damping, and correlated noise generated self-consistently by the interacting electronic subsystem. Applying the approach to a minimal two-orbital model containing the two symmetry-distinct distortions of the monoclinic phase of vanadium dioxide, we find that the two distortions melt at well separated temperatures, giving an insulating, an intermediate metallic, and a high-symmetry phase. The two transitions have different origins: the melting of the dimerization is driven by the coupling to the correlated electrons and is captured already within the coherent lattice approximation, whereas the restoration of the undistorted structure requires the entropy of the fluctuating lattice. Since the electronic subsystem is treated within nonequilibrium dynamical mean-field theory, the framework can be extended in future work to photoexcited systems with nonthermal electronic distributions.

</details>


### [170] [Ginzburg-Landau Theory for Non-Invertible Symmetry-Breaking Transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06751)
*Vibhu Ravindran,Luisa Eck,Xie Chen*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper develops a generalized Ginzburg-Landau field theory for unconventional phase transitions understood as symmetry-breaking of non-invertible symmetries, using the Symmetry Topological Field Theory formalism to derive local order parameters from a (2+1)D topological order, illustrated with transitions between gapped phases under the Rep(S_3) symmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The generalized Landau paradigm explains deconfined or topological transitions in (1+1)D as symmetry-breaking of non-invertible symmetries, but universal dynamical properties of these transitions are not yet accessible. The authors seek a field-theoretic framework, following Ginzburg-Landau philosophy, to describe critical points via fluctuations of local order parameters of non-invertible symmetries.

Method: The paper employs the Symmetry Topological Field Theory formalism to identify the algebra of local order parameters via the Lagrangian algebra of a (2+1)D topological order. It develops a generalized Ginzburg-Landau procedure and illustrates it with the concrete example of transitions between gapped phases protected by the Rep(S_3) non-invertible symmetry.

Result: The paper demonstrates a systematic procedure to construct a field-theoretic description of critical points associated with non-invertible symmetry-breaking transitions, using the Lagrangian algebra data from the Symmetry Topological Field Theory, and works through the full steps for the Rep(S_3) example.

Conclusion: The work extends the Ginzburg-Landau framework beyond conventional symmetry-breaking transitions to encompass topological/deconfined transitions via non-invertible symmetries, validated on a concrete Rep(S_3)-symmetric example.

Abstract: Recently, a generalized Landau paradigm has been proposed, in which a broad class of "unconventional" phase transitions lying beyond the original symmetry-breaking framework of Landau are understood as the symmetry-breaking transitions of generalized symmetries. In particular, in (1+1)D, such "topological" or "deconfined" transitions can be mapped to the symmetry-breaking transitions of non-invertible symmetries. How to extract the universal dynamical properties of the transitions from this understanding? In this paper, we follow the Ginzburg-Landau philosophy and develop a field-theoretic description of such critical points in terms of the fluctuations of local order parameters, now of non-invertible symmetries. The Symmetry Topological Field Theory formalism plays a central role in our analysis, allowing us to identify the algebra of local order parameters from the Lagrangian algebra of a (2+1)D topological order. We illustrate the key ideas and the full steps of this generalized Ginzburg-Landau procedure using the transitions between gapped phases with a simple non-invertible symmetry --- the Rep($S_3$) symmetry.

</details>


### [171] [Spin--orbit-enhanced correlation sensitivity and anomalous magnetic response in non-dimerized $4d^4$ ilmenite CdRuO$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.06902)
*Yuya Haraguchi,Hayato Yatsuzuka,Hiroko Aruga Katori*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: CdRuO$_3$, a nominal Ru$^{4+}$ 4d$^4$ ilmenite with edge-sharing RuO$_6$ honeycomb layers, crystallizes in a non-dimerized $R\bar{3}$ structure with equivalent Ru-Ru bonds but a strongly distorted RuO$_6$ environment. It exhibits nonmetallic, non-Arrhenius resistivity and a finite residual linear heat capacity term. DFT calculations show PBE+$U$ stays metallic/semimetallic up to U$_{eff}$ = 3 eV, whereas PBE+SOC+$U$ opens a ~55 meV direct band gap at Γ for U$_{eff}$ = 2.5 eV, indicating spin-orbit coupling strongly enhances correlation sensitivity. After removing dilute Curie-Weiss defect contributions, the susceptibility is weakly nonmonotonic, inconsistent with both Pauli behavior and independent S=1 moments. CdRuO$_3$ realizes the non-dimerized structural branch predicted for ruthenium ilmenites but is not a simple robust multiorbital metal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To synthesize and characterize CdRuO$_3$, an unexplored ruthenium ilmenite, in order to test predictions regarding the non-dimerized structural branch and to understand the interplay of spin-orbit coupling and electronic correlations in 4d$^4$ honeycomb systems.

Method: Powder X-ray diffraction for structural determination; compacted-pellet resistivity and heat capacity measurements for electronic characterization; magnetic susceptibility measurements with Curie-Weiss defect subtraction; and matched nonmagnetic DFT calculations comparing PBE+$U$ with and without spin-orbit coupling across U$_{eff}$ values.

Result: A non-dimerized $R\bar{3}$ structure with equivalent Ru-Ru bonds was established. The material shows nonmetallic, non-Arrhenius resistivity and a finite residual linear heat capacity term. PBE+$U$ remains metallic/semimetallic up to U$_{eff}$ = 3 eV, while PBE+SOC+$U$ opens a ~55 meV direct gap at Γ for U$_{eff}$ = 2.5 eV. The susceptibility is weakly nonmonotonic and inconsistent with both Pauli and spin-only S=1 responses.

Conclusion: CdRuO$_3$ realizes the predicted non-dimerized structural branch of ruthenium ilmenites but is not a simple robust multiorbital metal; spin-orbit coupling markedly enhances the correlation sensitivity of the non-cubic Ru $t_{2g}$ manifold, placing this system near an electronic instability.

Abstract: We report the synthesis and physical properties of CdRuO$_3$, a nominal Ru$^{4+}$ $4d^4$ ilmenite with edge-sharing RuO$_6$ honeycomb layers. Powder X-ray diffraction establishes a crystallographically non-dimerized $R\bar{3}$ structure with equivalent Ru-Ru bonds and a strongly distorted RuO$_6$ environment. The compacted-pellet resistivity is nonmetallic but non-Arrhenius, while the heat capacity contains a finite residual linear term. Matched nonmagnetic calculations show that PBE+$U$ without spin-orbit coupling remains metallic or semimetallic up to $U_{\mathrm{eff}} = 3$ eV, whereas PBE+SOC+$U$ exhibits a strong $U_{\mathrm{eff}}$ dependence and opens a direct gap of approximately 55 meV at $Γ$ for $U_{\mathrm{eff}} = 2.5$ eV. Spin-orbit coupling therefore markedly enhances the correlation sensitivity of the non-cubic Ru $t_{2g}$ manifold. After subtraction of a dilute Curie-Weiss defect contribution, the susceptibility remains weakly nonmonotonic and is inconsistent with both an ordinary Pauli response and independent spin-only $S = 1$ moments. CdRuO$_3$ thus realizes the non-dimerized structural branch predicted for ruthenium ilmenites, but not a simple robust multiorbital metal.

</details>


### [172] [Raman signatures of a non-reciprocal magnetic phase transition in Ca$_2$RuO$_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07565)
*Giacomo Jarc,Giovanni Tartaglia,Francesco Gabriele,Filomena Forte,Anita Guarino,Angela Montanaro,Enrico Maria Rigoni,Nitesh Khatiwada,Costanza Lincetto,Gabriele Bartolini,Antonio Mastropasqua,Shahla Yasmin Mathengattil,Marco Malvestuto,Muhammad Waqee Ur Rehman,Rosalba Fittipaldi,Joachim Deisenhofer,Alexander A. Tsirlin,Antonio Vecchione,Mario Cuoco,Daniele Fausti*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper reports Raman spectroscopy evidence of a magnetic first-order phase transition in Ca2RuO4 driven by an out-of-plane magnetic field along the crystallographic c-axis. The transition is marked by a magnetic-origin mode and modified phonon-Higgs amplitude mode coupling, both showing non-reciprocal hysteresis. The dipolar antiferromagnetic order remains intact, suggesting a new quadrupolar field-controlled state that hybridizes the lattice and magnetic Higgs modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ca2RuO4 exhibits a complex Mott insulating state with anomalous antiferromagnetic order and a collective amplitude (Higgs-like) magnetic excitation. Understanding how magnetic fields tune this state is important for unraveling the interplay between strong correlations, magneto-elastic coupling, and spin-orbit effects in this material.

Method: Raman scattering measurements were performed on Ca2RuO4 as a function of an out-of-plane magnetic field applied along the c-axis. The evolution of magnetic-origin modes, phonon modes, and their couplings was tracked across the field-induced transition, including analysis of magnetic-field hysteresis.

Result: The measurements reveal a magnetic first-order phase transition characterized by the emergence of a magnetic-origin Raman mode and a modification of phonon-Higgs amplitude mode coupling, both displaying non-reciprocal hysteresis in magnetic field. Notably, in-plane magnon Raman scattering is unaffected, indicating preservation of the dipolar antiferromagnetic order.

Conclusion: The observed transition is consistent with the onset of a new quadrupolar field-controlled state in which fluctuations hybridize the lattice with the magnetic Higgs mode, explaining the new Raman features while leaving the existing antiferromagnetic dipolar order intact.

Abstract: The magnetic behavior of Ca$_2$RuO$_4$ represents a unique puzzle due to the interplay of strong electronic correlations, magneto-elastic interactions, and large spin-orbit coupling. At low temperatures, an anomalous Mott insulating state emerges, characterized by a complex antiferromagnetic order with a collective amplitude excitation of the magnetic moment which has been discussed in analogy with the Higgs mode. We report here evidence of a magnetic first-order phase transition driven by an out-of-plane magnetic field along the crystallographic $c$-axis of Ca$_2$RuO$_4$. Raman measurements in magnetic field reveal the emergence of mode of magnetic origin and a concomitant modification of the coupling between a phonon and the Higgs amplitude mode. Both the Raman features are characterized by a non-reciprocal hysteresis in magnetic field. Surprisingly, the observed phase transition does not affect the Raman scattering from the in-plane magnons, indicating that the dipolar antiferromagnetic order is preserved. This is consistent with the onset of a new quadrupolar field-controlled state, whose fluctuations structure can trigger an hybridization between the lattice and the magnetic Higgs mode resulting in the observed Raman features.

</details>


### [173] [Exact fermionic dual of the Bose-Hubbard model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07830)
*Lei Su,Ivar Martin,Aashish A. Clerk*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper establishes an exact fermionic gauging duality (generalized Jordan-Wigner transformation) for the Bose-Hubbard model with global U(1) symmetry, yielding a dual description in terms of fermionic composites. This generalizes the exact 1D hard-core BH to Fermi-Hubbard mapping to soft-core interactions, reducing to a sine-Gordon/Thirring equivalence at low energies, and is verified with DMRG.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend exact bosonization/fermionization dualities beyond Z2-symmetric systems to models with continuous U(1) symmetry, thereby obtaining an exact fermionic dual of the Bose-Hubbard model that captures soft-core interactions and provides new insights into strongly correlated Bose-Fermi systems.

Method: The authors apply the recently developed framework of fermionic gauging (realizing generalized Jordan-Wigner transformations) to the Bose-Hubbard model with global U(1) symmetry. They derive an exact dual description in terms of fermionic composites constructed from bosons and fermions. The low-energy limit is analyzed via the mapping between sine-Gordon and Thirring models, and numerical verification is performed using density matrix renormalization group (DMRG) calculations. The construction is naturally extended to generic bosonic systems and higher dimensions.

Result: The duality is verified with DMRG, showing the gapless phase and the phase transition are governed by the compact boson conformal field theory. A novel manifestation of Luttinger's theorem is identified: the oscillation wave vector of the fermionic composite correlation function in the gapless phase is fixed by their density. In 1D the mapping generalizes the extended hard-core BH to spinless Fermi-Hubbard equivalence to include soft-core interactions.

Conclusion: The work establishes an exact fermionic dual for the Bose-Hubbard model, unifies known low-energy equivalences, provides a new signature of Luttinger's theorem in composite fermion correlations, and opens routes to study Bose-Fermi mixtures in optical lattices and other strongly correlated quantum systems, with natural extensions to higher dimensions.

Abstract: Recent developments have established exact bosonization and fermionization with a $\mathbb{Z}_2$ symmetry as dualities through gauging. In this work, we apply fermionic gauging, which realizes generalized Jordan-Wigner transformations, to the Bose-Hubbard (BH) model with a global $U(1)$ symmetry and derive an exact dual description in terms of fermionic composites, built from bosons and fermions. In 1D, this duality generalizes the exact mapping between the extended hard-core BH model and the spinless Fermi-Hubbard model to now cover soft-core interactions. At low energies, the mapping reduces to the well-known equivalence between the sine-Gordon model and the Thirring model. The oscillation wave vector of the fermionic composite correlation function in the gapless phase is fixed by their density, providing a novel manifestation of Luttinger's theorem. We verify the exact duality using density matrix renormalization group (DMRG) calculations and demonstrate that the gapless phase and the phase transition are governed by the compact boson conformal field theory. Our construction naturally extends to generic bosonic systems and higher dimensions, opening new avenues for studying Bose-Fermi mixtures in optical lattices and other strongly correlated quantum systems.

</details>


### [174] [Hysteretic Coherence Collapse Across the First Order CDW Transition in 1T-TaS2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07923)
*Turgut Yilmaz,Anil Rajapitamahuni,Asish K. Kundu,Menka Jain,Elio Vescovo*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Using ARPES, the study reveals that the in-gap state in the C-CDW phase of 1T-TaS2 undergoes abrupt collapse upon heating into the NC-CDW phase and re-emerges upon cooling, with pronounced thermal hysteresis providing direct spectroscopic evidence of the first-order C-CDW/NC-CDW transition. The in-gap state's behavior closely tracks resistivity changes, linking it to electronic reconstruction across the transition.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The first-order phase transition between NC-CDW and C-CDW phases in 1T-TaS2 underpins its exotic electronic behavior, yet the spectroscopic evolution of the low-energy electronic structure across this transition remains poorly understood.

Method: Angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES) including photon-energy-dependent measurements to distinguish low-energy features, and temperature-dependent ARPES across both heating and cooling cycles to track spectral evolution across the phase transition.

Result: Two low-energy features (a flat band associated with the lower Hubbard band and an in-gap state closer to the Fermi level) are distinguished by their spectral weight evolution in photon-energy dependent measurements. The in-gap state collapses abruptly upon heating into the NC-CDW phase and re-emerges sharply upon cooling into the C-CDW phase, exhibiting pronounced thermal hysteresis that confirms the first-order nature of the transition. Its disappearance/recovery tracks resistivity changes closely.

Conclusion: The observed thermal hysteresis provides direct spectroscopic evidence of the first-order C-CDW/NC-CDW transition, and the in-gap state's correlation with resistivity behavior highlights its intimate connection to the electronic reconstruction across this phase transition.

Abstract: The first order phase transition between the nearly commensurate (NC-CDW) and commensurate (C-CDW) charge density wave phases in 1T-TaS2 underpins its exotic electronic behavior, yet the spectroscopic evolution of the low energy electronic structure across this transition remains crucial to understand. Using angle resolved photoemission spectroscopy (ARPES), we investigate the low temperature C-CDW phase, characterized by a flat band commonly associated with the lower Hubbard band and a distinct in-gap state located closer to the Fermi level. Photon energy dependent measurements distinguish these two low energy features through their different spectral weight evolution. Temperature dependent ARPES across heating and cooling cycles reveals that the in-gap state undergoes an abrupt collapse upon heating into the NC-CDW phase and re-emerges sharply upon cooling back into the C-CDW phase. This pronounced thermal hysteresis provides direct spectroscopic evidence of the first order nature of the transition. Furthermore, the disappearance and recovery of the in-gap state closely track the corresponding changes in resistivity, highlighting its intimate connection to the electronic reconstruction across the C-CDW/NC-CDW phase transition.

</details>


### [175] [Vertical quantum confinement in bulk MoS$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07950)
*Jairo Obando-Guevara,Álvaro González-García,Marcin Rosmus,Natalia Olszowska,César González,Miguel Ángel González-Barrio,Antonio Tejeda,Arantzazu Mascaraque*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper experimentally observes quantum confinement states (quantum well states) in bulk MoS2 using ARPES, showing vertical quantum confinement of electrons with no kz dispersion and strong photon energy intensity modulation. The binding energy of these QWSs varies linearly with quantum number n (not quadratically as in a 2DEG), consistent with a parabolic-like quantum well. This confinement stems from mechanical exfoliation detaching a multilayer stack from the bulk, confirmed by DFT calculations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore and understand quantum confinement phenomena in bulk-like MoS2, which could enable studying intersubband transitions for optical applications and fundamental quantum phenomena in multilayer transition metal dichalcogenide stacks of varying thicknesses.

Method: Angle-Resolved Photoemission Spectroscopy (ARPES) was used to probe the band structure at the Γ point in bulk MoS2, examining kz dispersion and photon energy dependence. Density Functional Theory (DFT) calculations were employed to confirm the origin of the confinement arising from mechanical exfoliation-induced multilayer stack detachment.

Result: Quantum well states were observed at the Γ point with no dispersion in kz and strong intensity modulation with photon energy, confirming vertical quantum confinement. The QWS binding energy vs quantum number n showed a linear dependence (not the quadratic 2DEG behavior), consistent with a parabolic-like quantum well. The confinement was attributed to multilayer detachment via mechanical exfoliation, as confirmed by DFT.

Conclusion: Quantum confinement can be realized in bulk-like MoS2, offering opportunities to study intersubband transitions for optical applications and fundamental quantum phenomena in multilayer stacks of different thicknesses.

Abstract: We experimentally observe quantum confinement states in bulk MoS$_2$ using Angle-Resolved Photoemission Spectroscopy (ARPES). The band structure at the $\overlineΓ$ point reveals quantum well states (QWSs) linked to vertical quantum confinement of the electrons, confirmed by the absence of dispersion in k$_z$ and a strong intensity modulation with photon energy. Notably, the binding energy dependence of the QWSs vs $n$ does not follow the quadratic dependence of a two-dimensional electron gas. Instead, a linear behaviour is observed that is consistent with a parabolic-like quantum well. This confinement arises from the mechanical exfoliation preparation method, which leads to the detachment of a multilayer stack from the underlying bulk. This is confirmed by Density Functional Theory (DFT) calculations. The quantum confinement in bulk-like MoS$_2$ not only offers the opportunity to explore intersubband transitions to exploit optical properties but also provides a means to study fundamental quantum phenomena in multilayer stacks of different thicknesses.

</details>


### [176] [Selective coupling of high-order phonons in La2-xSrxCuO4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08076)
*Ke-Jun Xu,Paulina Majchrzak,Nathan Giles-Donovan,Sangheon Kim,Jaewon Choi,Jun Okamoto,Ganesha Channagowdra,Hsiao-Yu Huang,Di-Jing Huang,Seiki Komiya,Takao Sasagawa,Shuichi Wakimoto,Wei-Sheng Lee,Zhi-Xun Shen,Thomas P. Devereaux,Dung-Hai Lee,Robert J. Birgeneau*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Using resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS) on La2-xSrxCuO4, the authors discover sharp excitations at ~85, 180, and 330 meV in the undoped compound that are consistent with one-, two-, and four-phonon excitations of the in-plane Cu-O bond stretching mode. These excitations show no observable dispersion, selectively couple to even-ordered phonons, exhibit a stark contrast with Raman spectra, and vanish at 10% hole doping. This indicates anomalous electron-phonon coupling beyond the Franck-Condon picture, possibly implying locally paired quasiparticles in insulating cuprates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover a new regime of electron-phonon coupling in cuprates by examining high-order harmonic vibrational excitations in La2-xSrxCuO4, which deviate from the conventional Franck-Condon intensity envelope and challenge existing theoretical pictures of electron-phonon interaction in strongly correlated systems.

Method: The study employs resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS) on La2-xSrxCuO4 with varying hole doping levels, measuring the energy positions and intensities of vibrational excitations, and comparing them with Raman spectroscopy data and known phonon energies.

Result: Undoped La2CuO4 shows sharp peaks at approximately 85 meV, 180 meV, and 330 meV with no dispersion, where the higher two energies match expected two-phonon and four-phonon excitations of the in-plane Cu-O stretching mode. These excitations show strong doping dependence, becoming unresolvable at 10% hole doping where plasmons dominate low-energy excitations. The coupling is selective to even-ordered phonons, contrasting sharply with Raman spectra.

Conclusion: The observations reveal anomalous electron-phonon coupling beyond the Franck-Condon framework. A possible explanation is the existence of locally paired quasiparticles in insulating cuprates, which could have important implications for understanding pairing mechanisms in high-temperature superconductivity.

Abstract: In crystals and molecules with strong coupling between the charge carriers and atomic vibrations, high order harmonics of the vibrational excitations can be observed with intensity following the Franck-Condon envelope. Here, we uncover a new regime of electron-phonon coupling in La2-xSrxCuO4 using resonant inelastic X-ray scattering. In the undoped compound, we find sharp peaks at approximately 85$\pm$5 meV, 180$\pm$7 meV, and 330$\pm$5 meV with no observable dispersion, and an absence of appreciable intensity from other modes observed in Raman spectroscopy in this energy range. The 180 meV and 330 meV excitations have energies approximately consistent with two-phonon and four-phonon in-plane Cu-O bond stretching mode, suggesting that the resonantly excited valence electrons strongly couple to this mode. These excitations exhibit significant doping dependence, eventually becoming unresolvable at 10% hole doping, where the low energy charge excitations are dominated by dispersive plasmons. The observation of selective coupling to even-ordered phonons and stark contrast with the Raman spectra indicate anomalous electron-phonon coupling beyond the Franck-Condon picture. One intriguing possibility is the existence of locally paired quasiparticles in insulating cuprates.

</details>


### [177] [Textures as a phase-transition probe for quantum spin chains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08120)
*Heitor P. Casagrande,Isaac M. Carvalho,William J. Munro,Krissia Zawadzki*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper studies the use of "quantum texture"—specifically the rugosity metric derived from it—as a tool for probing quantum phase transitions in the Heisenberg XXZ model. The authors establish a link between rugosity in the computational basis and the hierarchy of spin correlators, and analyze both global and symmetry-resolved rugosities across different magnetization sectors. They find distinct rugosity signatures at both transition points of the XXZ model, with a sharp feature appearing at Δ=1 (the isotropic SU(2) point coinciding with the BKT transition) even for small systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantum texture was previously introduced as a tool for quantifying coherences and quantum gate identification. This work extends its application to quantum phase transitions, proposing the rugosity metric as a simple yet effective probe for phase-transition detection, building on the need for accessible diagnostic tools in many-body physics.

Method: The authors define rugosity in the computational basis and establish its connection to the hierarchy of spin correlators. They compute both global rugosities (from the full ground state) and symmetry-resolved rugosities (from ground states within specific magnetization sectors) to probe the phase diagram of the Heisenberg XXZ model, examining signatures at its phase transition points.

Result: Distinct rugosity signatures are observed at both transition points of the XXZ model. Notably, a sharp feature appears at Δ=1 already for small systems, indicating pronounced sensitivity of the correlation hierarchy (encoded by the texture) to the isotropic point. This may reflect particular sensitivity of rugosity to the structure of the spin-correlation hierarchy at SU(2) isotropy.

Conclusion: The rugosity metric derived from quantum texture serves as an effective and simple tool for probing quantum phase transitions, offering sensitivity to the spin-correlation hierarchy—especially at the isotropic point where the BKT transition occurs in the XXZ model.

Abstract: The idea of quantum texture has been recently proposed and used as a tool for quantifying coherences and for quantum gate identification. In this work we offer a study on its usage to quantum phase transitions, demonstrating the rugosity metric as a simple tool for effective phase-transition probing. We establish the link between rugosity in the computational basis and the hierarchy of spin correlators, and analyze rugosities defined in the global ground-state and in ground-states belonging to different magnetization sectors (to which we refer to as global vs symmetry-resolved rugosities) to study the phase diagram of the Heisenberg XXZ model. We find distinct rugosity signatures at both transition points. In particular, a sharp feature appears at $Δ=1$ already for small systems, revealing a pronounced sensitivity of the correlation hierarchy encoded by the texture to this point. Since the BKT transition coincides with the isotropic $SU(2)$ point of the XXZ model, this behavior may reflect a particular sensitivity of rugosity to the structure of the spin-correlation hierarchy at isotropy.

</details>


### [178] [Observation of multipartite spin entanglement in a cuprate chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08134)
*S. F. R. TenHuisen,Z. Shen,V. Bhartiya,V. Menon,P. Sharma,H. Padma,Z. Guan,W. He,M. K. Lajer,J. Li,D. Banerjee,J. Pelliciari,I. A. Zaliznyak,G. D. Gu,M. D. Lukin,S. Johnston,M. P. M. Dean,V. Bisogni,Y. Wang,M. Mitrano*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS) can be used to directly extract the Quantum Fisher Information from measured spin fluctuation spectra, enabling detection of at least 7-partite spin entanglement in the one-dimensional cuprate Sr2CuO3—one of the largest entanglement depths reported in a solid—with results consistent with Hubbard-model predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Quantum materials are believed to harbor highly entangled states of matter, but quantifying such many-body entanglement experimentally has remained a long-standing challenge, motivating the need for a practical experimental probe.

Method: The authors use resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS) as a probe of multipartite spin entanglement, projecting the RIXS cross section onto the appropriate spin excitation channel to extract the Quantum Fisher Information directly from the measured spin fluctuation spectrum.

Result: The method detects at least 7-partite spin entanglement in the one-dimensional cuprate Sr2CuO3, achieving one of the largest entanglement depths yet reported in a solid. The observed entanglement depth agrees with Hubbard-model predictions and persists up to elevated temperatures.

Conclusion: RIXS is established as an experimental probe of many-body spin entanglement, providing a platform for future extensions to charge and orbital sectors as well as nonequilibrium settings.

Abstract: Quantum materials are believed to host highly entangled states of matter, but probing and quantifying such entanglement has long remained experimentally elusive. Here, we use resonant inelastic X-ray scattering (RIXS) as a probe of multipartite spin entanglement. By projecting the RIXS cross section onto the appropriate spin excitation channel, we show that the Quantum Fisher Information can be extracted directly from the measured spin fluctuation spectrum. We detect at least 7-partite spin entanglement in the model one-dimensional cuprate Sr$_2$CuO$_3$, revealing one of the largest entanglement depths yet reported in a solid. The observed entanglement depth agrees with Hubbard-model expectations and persists to elevated temperatures. Our results establish RIXS as an experimental probe of many-body spin entanglement and provide a platform for extensions to charge and orbital sectors and nonequilibrium settings.

</details>


### [179] [Successive Phase Transitions from a Composite Fermion Liquid to a Fractional Quantum Hall State at ν=3/2 Driven by In-Plane Magnetic Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08141)
*Xinghao Wang,L. N. Pfeiffer,A. Gupta,K. W. Baldwin,K. W. West,Rui-Rui Du*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: An even-denominator fractional quantum Hall (FQH) state at ν = 3/2 is induced purely by an in-plane magnetic field in an ultra-high-mobility GaAs quantum well. The system undergoes two successive transitions—first from a composite fermion liquid to a soft-gap FQH state at ~12.2 T, then via a topological phase transition to a hard-gap robust FQH state at ~14.7 T—accompanied by a daughter state at ν = 19/13. The work demonstrates that topological order can be engineered via k-space Fermi contour splitting under an in-plane magnetic field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore a new mechanism for realizing even-denominator fractional quantum Hall states (potentially hosting non-Abelian quasiparticles), specifically by using an in-plane magnetic field to induce k-space Fermi contour splitting as a control knob for engineering topological order—rather than relying on conventional perpendicular-field or sample-dependent approaches.

Method: Magnetotransport measurements in an ultra-high-mobility GaAs quantum well with a systematically varied in-plane magnetic field B_||. The evolution of the state at ν = 3/2 is tracked as a function of B_||, revealing two successive transitions and the emergence of a daughter state at ν = 19/13, alongside soft- and hard-gap FQH regimes.

Result: An even-denominator FQH state at ν = 3/2 emerges solely from in-plane magnetic field application. Two transitions are observed: at B_|| ≈ 12.2 T (composite fermion liquid to soft-gap FQH) and at B_|| ≈ 14.7 T (topological phase transition to a robust hard-gap FQH state), with a daughter state at ν = 19/13 appearing as well.

Conclusion: In-plane magnetic field provides a novel, tunable route to engineer topological order in GaAs quantum wells through k-space Fermi contour splitting, offering a new platform for studying and controlling even-denominator fractional quantum Hall states and potentially non-Abelian anyons.

Abstract: We report an even-denominator fractional quantum Hall state at ν = 3/2 induced entirely by in-plane magnetic field B_|| in an ultra-high-mobility GaAs quantum well. As B_|| increases, the system undergoes two successive transitions: from a composite fermion liquid to a soft-gap FQH state (B_||~12.2 T), then via a topological phase transition to a hard-gap robust FQH state (B_||~14.7 T), accompanied by a daughter state at ν = 19/13. We present systematic data, and discuss a possible scenario in interpreting these findings. Our work demonstrates that topological order may be engineered through k-space Fermi contour splitting under an in-plane magnetic field.

</details>


### [180] [Kane-Lubensky phonons in Maxwell lattice frustrated Mott insulators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08262)
*Hong-Hao Song,Gang v. Chen*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper shows that gapless Weyl-line phonons (of the Kane-Lubensky type) can emerge in 3D Maxwell-lattice frustrated Mott insulators via magneto-topological mechanics. In a pyrochlore antiferromagnet with spin-lattice coupling, a magnetic field drives the system into a P4₃32 topological Maxwell lattice hosting bulk zero-frequency Weyl-line phonons — distinct from Goldstone modes — leading to a characteristic C_ph ~ T² phonon specific heat, with potential applications in Cr-based spinels and beyond.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To discover a mechanism for realizing topological zero-energy (gapless) phonon modes in realistic 3D frustrated magnetic materials, and to establish a platform where topological zero-frequency phonons couple strongly to other degrees of freedom, enabling exotic phonon-mediated phenomena.

Method: The authors consider a pyrochlore antiferromagnet with spin-lattice coupling. A magnetic field selects a particular spin state whose induced lattice distortion yields a P4₃32 topological lattice. They show that the spin-lattice coupling plus spin configuration bends neighboring bonds, converting the system into a topological Maxwell lattice. The resulting bulk zero-frequency Weyl-line phonons are shown to be topologically protected and distinct from Goldstone modes. The low-temperature phonon specific heat is then derived from the one-dimensional zero-mode manifolds.

Result: The system hosts topological bulk zero-frequency Weyl-line phonons that are not Goldstone modes. This yields a characteristic low-temperature phonon specific heat scaling as C_ph ~ T² (contrasting with the conventional C_ph ~ T³ for Goldstone phonons). Potential applications in Cr-based spinel systems are identified.

Conclusion: The work establishes a low-energy platform where an extensive number of topological zero-frequency phonons can strongly couple to other degrees of freedom, opening a route to exotic phonon-mediated phenomena in frustrated Mott insulators.

Abstract: We show that zero-energy gapless Weyl-line phonons of the Kane-Lubensky's type can arise in the three-dimensional Maxwell lattice frustrated Mott insulators through the magnetopological mechanics. In a pyrochlore antiferromagnet with the spin-lattice coupling, a magnetic field selects the spin state whose lattice distortion generates a $P4_3 32$ topological lattice. More crucially, the spin-lattice coupling and the spin configuration cause the bending of the neighbouring bonds, and converts the system into the topological Maxwell lattice. Remarkably, the resulting system is found to host the bulk zero-frequency Weyl-line phonons protected topologically, and these gapless phonons are not Goldstone modes. Unlike the conventional ${C_{\rm ph}\sim T^3}$ for the Goldstone phonons, these one-dimensional zero-mode manifolds yield a characteristic low-temperature phonon specific heat ${C_{\rm ph}\sim T^2}$. Our results could find applications in the Cr-based spinel systems, and moreover, we establish a low-energy platform where an extensive number of topological zero-frequency phonons can strongly couple to other degrees of freedom, opening a route to exotic phonon-mediated phenomena.

</details>


### [181] [Theory of Kondo hybridization wave in Kondo lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08467)
*Yin Zhong*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper develops a microscopic theory for spatially modulated Kondo hybridization wave (KHW) order in heavy fermion systems, based on the square-lattice Kondo lattice model within large-N fermionic mean-field theory. The theory successfully reproduces the spatial modulation patterns observed in UTe2 and 1T/1H-TaS2 and predicts a transport anisotropy fingerprint for distinguishing KHW states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent STM experiments discovered spatially modulated Kondo hybridization wave order in UTe2 and 1T/1H-TaS2, which challenges the conventional assumption of spatially uniform Kondo hybridization in heavy fermion physics. A microscopic theoretical framework is needed to explain these unexpected observations.

Method: The authors develop a microscopic theory using the canonical square-lattice Kondo lattice model under the large-N fermionic mean-field approximation. They systematically explore modulated KHW phases, construct the ground-state phase diagram, and analyze the spectroscopic and transport properties of the identified phases.

Result: The theory identifies stable modulated KHW phases with a prominent Q=(0,π) phase that yields uniaxial stripe modulation of the Kondo hybridization gap, reproducing the spatial pattern in UTe2 and the unit-cell-doubling spectroscopic features in 1T/1H-TaS2. The predicted in-plane conductivity anisotropy provides a definitive transport fingerprint for distinguishing KHW states with different ordering wavevectors.

Conclusion: The work provides a microscopic foundation for the newly observed KHW order and establishes a unified theoretical framework for understanding emergent modulated hybridization phenomena in heavy fermion materials.

Abstract: Recent scanning tunneling microscopy experiments have discovered emergent spatially modulated Kondo hybridization wave (KHW) order in the heavy fermion superconductor UTe$_2$ and the artificial Kondo lattice system 1T/1H-TaS$_2$, challenging the conventional paradigm of spatially uniform Kondo hybridization in heavy fermion physics. Here, we develop a microscopic theory for KHW order based on the canonical square-lattice Kondo lattice model within the large-$N$ fermionic mean-field approximation. We systematically identify stable modulated KHW phases and establish their ground-state phase diagram. The prominent $\boldsymbol{Q}=(0,π)$ KHW phase yields uniaxial stripe modulation of the Kondo hybridization gap, which faithfully reproduces the spatial modulation pattern observed in UTe$_2$. Moreover, its inherent unit-cell-doubling modulation precisely accounts for the spectroscopic features measured in 1T/1H-TaS$_2$. We further predict a characteristic in-plane conductivity anisotropy that serves as a definitive transport fingerprint to discriminate KHW states with distinct ordering wavevectors. Our work provides a microscopic foundation for the newly observed KHW order and establishes a unified theoretical framework for understanding emergent modulated hybridization phenomena in heavy fermion materials.

</details>


### [182] [High-resolution angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy with tunable magnetic field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08498)
*Jairo Obando-Guevara,Huu-Thong Le,Emiliano Corcino Aracena,Sayan Singha,Kyungchan Lee,Yingdong Guan,Zhiqiang Mao,Dezhe Jin,Chaoxing Liu,Heike Pfau*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that electronic structure can be recovered from magneto-ARPES spectra with high accuracy and high momentum resolution even under strong electron trajectory aberrations from magnetic fields. Using Bi₂Se₃ in a dipole field with a laser-based ARPES system, the authors reconstruct the electronic structure via post-processing with detailed electron trajectory simulations, and identify key technical requirements—2D momentum mapping, micron beam spot size, and precise numerical field simulations—critical for high-resolution measurements. They also show circular dichroism can probe magnetic-field-spin coupling, and provide scaling laws toward magneto-ARPES in larger fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Magnetic field control of quantum phenomena is essential in materials research, and while in-situ field tuning (magneto-ARPES) provides direct momentum- and energy-resolved electronic structure information, trajectory aberrations cause severe spectral distortions and broadening even in small fields. The motivation is to overcome these aberrations to enable high-resolution magneto-ARPES measurements.

Method: The authors studied Bi₂Se₃ in the dipole field of a coil using a laser-based ARPES system. The electronic structure was reconstructed in post-processing by simulating detailed electron trajectories with precise numerical field models, correcting for trajectory aberrations and restoring spectra to high accuracy and momentum resolution.

Result: Electronic structure was successfully recovered from magneto-ARPES spectra with high accuracy and maintained high momentum resolution despite strong trajectory aberrations. Two-dimensional momentum mapping, micron beam spot size, and precise numerical field simulations were identified as critical technical requirements. Circular dichroism was shown to provide insight into magnetic-field coupling to spin, and scaling laws derived from simulations chart a path toward larger-field magneto-ARPES.

Conclusion: High-resolution magneto-ARPES is achievable despite large electron trajectory distortions through post-processing reconstruction based on accurate field simulations. The identified requirements and scaling laws provide a roadmap for extending magneto-ARPES to stronger fields, enabling field-dependent electronic structure studies with high momentum resolution.

Abstract: The control and perturbation of quantum phenomena with magnetic fields is an indispensable tool in materials research. Recently, in-situ field tuning was implemented into angle-resolved photoemission spectroscopy (magneto-ARPES), which provides direct momentum and energy-resolved information of the field-dependent electronic structure. However, aberrations of the electron trajectories were shown to be substantial, leading to significant spectral distortions and broadening even in small fields. Here we show that the electronic structure can be recovered from magneto-ARPES spectra with high accuracy while maintaining high momentum resolution even when strong trajectory aberrations are present. We studied Bi$_2$Se$_3$ in a dipole field of a coil using a laser-based ARPES system. The electronic structure was reconstructed in post-processing using detailed electron trajectory simulations. We identify two-dimensional (2D) momentum mapping, a micron beam spot size, and precise numerical field simulations as critical technical requirements for high-resolution magneto-ARPES. We show how circular dichroism can provide additional information about the coupling of the magnetic field to the spin. The experimental achievements and the scaling laws from our simulations provide a road map towards magneto-ARPES in larger fields.

</details>


### [183] [Schwinger boson perturbation theory for spin-$S$ Kitaev-Heisenberg magnets: phase diagram and dynamical response near Kitaev spin liquids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08548)
*Daiki Sasamoto,Joji Nasu*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper develops a Schwinger boson perturbative framework for the spin-S Kitaev-Heisenberg model using the quantum spin-liquid saddle point of the pure Kitaev model as the unperturbed state. Using the Klein duality to decompose the Hamiltonian, random-phase approximation calculations determine phase boundaries for S=1/2, 1, 3/2, and 2, showing spin-liquid regions shrink rapidly with increasing spin. Spin dynamics for S=1 retain a broad two-spinon continuum at finite energies with softened low-energy spectral weight near magnetic ordering wave vectors, enabling thermodynamic-limit calculations of spin dynamics near magnetic instabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the Kitaev quantum spin liquid behaves and destabilizes as spin magnitude S increases beyond S=1/2, and to develop a theoretically controlled framework capable of computing spin dynamics near magnetic instabilities in the Kitaev-Heisenberg model for arbitrary spin S.

Method: The authors construct a Schwinger boson perturbation theory around the spin-liquid saddle point of the pure Kitaev model. They exploit the Klein duality symmetry of the Kitaev-Heisenberg Hamiltonian to separate it into a duality-invariant part and a part that changes sign under the duality transformation. The duality-invariant part serves as the unperturbed Hamiltonian while the sign-changing part is treated as a perturbation within the random-phase approximation (RPA).

Result: Phase boundaries between quantum spin-liquid and magnetically ordered phases are determined for S=1/2, 1, 3/2, and 2. The spin-liquid regions shrink rapidly with increasing S, becoming very narrow at S=2. For S=1 spin dynamics, the dressed dynamical spin structure factor retains a broad two-spinon continuum at finite energies, while low-energy spectral weight softens at the ordering wave vectors of adjacent magnetic phases.

Conclusion: The Schwinger boson RPA framework provides a controlled method for studying the stability of the Kitaev quantum spin liquid and computing spin dynamics near magnetic instabilities for arbitrary spin S in the thermodynamic limit, revealing a systematic shrinkage of the spin-liquid phase with increasing spin.

Abstract: We develop a Schwinger boson perturbative framework for the spin-$S$ Kitaev-Heisenberg model. The spin-liquid saddle point of the pure Kitaev model is used as the unperturbed state, and magnetic instabilities and dynamical spin correlations are evaluated around this saddle point. We decompose the Hamiltonian exactly into two parts by exploiting the Klein duality intrinsic to the Kitaev-Heisenberg model. We perform the random-phase approximation by taking the part invariant under the duality transformation as the unperturbed term and treating the remaining term, which changes sign under it, as the perturbation. We determine the phase boundaries separating the quantum spin-liquid regimes from the adjacent magnetically ordered phases for $S=1/2$, $1$, $3/2$, and $2$. The spin-liquid regions shrink rapidly with increasing $S$ and become very narrow at $S=2$. We also compute the spin dynamics of the spin-$S$ Kitaev-Heisenberg model, focusing on $S=1$, and find that the dressed dynamical spin structure factor retains a broad two-spinon continuum at finite energies, while the low-energy spectral weight softens at the ordering wave vectors of the adjacent magnetic phases. Our framework thus enables thermodynamic-limit calculations of spin dynamics near magnetic instabilities.

</details>


### [184] [Magnetic phases of Kondo lattice materials Ce$_5$RhGe$_2$ and Ce$_5$IrGe$_2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08580)
*Jiawen Zhang,Yanan Zhang,Mingyi Wang,Ye Chen,Yu Liu,Yongjun Zhang,Michael Smidman,Huiqiu Yuan*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: Single crystals of Ce5RhGe2 and Ce5IrGe2 (alongside Ce5CoGe2) are systematically studied via resistivity, specific heat, and magnetization. All crystallize in orthorhombic Pnma with lattice parameters increasing from Co to Rh to Ir (negative chemical pressure). The a-axis is the easy magnetization direction across the series. Ce5RhGe2 orders ferromagnetically at TC≈11.5 K, resembling Ce5CoGe2, while Ce5IrGe2 shows two successive transitions at 12.7 K and 11.8 K, multiple metamagnetic transitions with magnetization plateaus at Ms/5 and Ms/3, and establishes a ferrimagnetic ground state at low temperatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically characterize the physical properties of Ce5RhGe2 and Ce5IrGe2 single crystals and understand how replacing Co with Rh and Ir (chemical/negative pressure effect) tunes magnetism across the Ce5TGe2 series, revealing a crossover from ferromagnetism to ferrimagnetism.

Method: Systematic measurements of electrical resistivity, specific heat, and magnetization along principal crystallographic axes on single crystals; structural characterization confirming the orthorhombic Pnma structure and lattice parameter evolution.

Result: Ce5RhGe2 is ferromagnetic at TC≈11.5 K, closely resembling Ce5CoGe2. Ce5IrGe2 shows two transitions (TM1=12.7 K, TM2=11.8 K), multiple metamagnetic transitions with plateaus near Ms/5 and Ms/3, and the easy-axis low-field metamagnetic transition shifts downward with decreasing temperature, eventually showing pronounced hysteresis about zero field—confirming a ferrimagnetic ground state.

Conclusion: The negative chemical pressure from expanding the lattice (Co→Rh→Ir) drives the series from ferromagnetic (Ce5CoGe2, Ce5RhGe2) to ferrimagnetic (Ce5IrGe2), revealing rich magnetic phase behavior and magnetization plateaus in the Ce5TGe2 family.

Abstract: Single crystals of Ce$_5$RhGe$_2$ and Ce$_5$IrGe$_2$ have been systematically investigated by electrical resistivity, specific heat, and magnetization measurements. Together with Ce$_5$CoGe$_2$, all three compounds crystallize in the orthorhombic \emph{Pnma} structure, with the lattice parameters increasing monotonically from Co to Rh to Ir, consistent with the effect of negative chemical pressure. Magnetization measurements along the three principal crystallographic axes identify the \emph{a} axis as the easy magnetization direction throughout the series. Ce$_5$RhGe$_2$ exhibits ferromagnetic ordering with a Curie temperature of approximately 11.5 K and shows magnetic behavior closely resembling that of Ce$_5$CoGe$_2$. In contrast, Ce$_5$IrGe$_2$ undergoes two successive magnetic transitions at $T_{\rm M1}=12.7$ K and $T_{\rm M2}=11.8$ K, and there are multiple metamagnetic transitions under magnetic fields, giving rise to magnetization plateaus at fractions of the saturation magnetization $M_{\rm s}$ of approximately $M_{\rm s}/5$ and $M_{\rm s}/3$. The low-field metamagnetic transition along the easy axis shifts to lower field with decreasing temperature, and eventually a pronounced hysteresis loop is observed about zero-field, establishing that Ce$_5$IrGe$_2$ exhibits a ferrimagnetic ground state at the lowest measured temperatures.

</details>


### [185] [$g$-wave altermagnetic order parameter in hematite](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08693)
*Tianren Wang,Yuehong Li,Yu Feng,Andong Liu,Yuetong Wu,Qian Zhao,Yujie Yan,Wei Luo,Xin Tong,Yi Lu,Yao Shen,Stefano Agrestini,Jaewon Choi,Qisi Wang*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper uses inelastic neutron scattering to quantitatively map the momentum-space symmetry of altermagnetic magnon splitting in hematite (α-Fe₂O₃), revealing g-wave symmetry where the splitting vanishes along nodal directions and peaks off-node, consistent with linear spin-wave theory and identifying opposite-chirality magnon branches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Altermagnets offer a unique combination of vanishing net magnetization and momentum-dependent spin splitting, with magnon band splitting serving as a direct probe of altermagnetic order. However, the quantitative momentum-space symmetry of this splitting has not yet been determined, motivating direct experimental measurement.

Method: Inelastic neutron scattering was employed to map the momentum dependence of the altermagnetic magnon splitting in hematite (α-Fe₂O₃), and the results were compared with linear spin-wave theory calculations based on the altermagnetic model to identify chirality and the underlying exchange interactions.

Result: The magnon splitting vanishes along nodal directions and reaches maxima off the nodes, revealing the g-wave symmetry of the altermagnetic order parameter. Linear spin-wave theory confirms the nondegenerate branches as magnons of opposite chirality and traces the splitting to symmetry-inequivalent long-range exchange interactions. This constitutes the first quantitative determination of the momentum-space symmetry of altermagnetic chiral magnons.

Conclusion: The quantitative determination of g-wave altermagnetic magnon symmetry, combined with hematite's high magnetic ordering temperature and low magnon damping, establishes it as a promising platform for low-dissipation, symmetry-selective magnonic applications.

Abstract: Altermagnets combine the vanishing net magnetization of antiferromagnets with momentum-dependent spin splitting. Magnon band splitting provides a direct probe of altermagnetic order and may enable chirality-selective magnon transport, yet the momentum-space symmetry of this splitting has not been determined quantitatively. Here we use inelastic neutron scattering to map the momentum dependence of altermagnetic magnon splitting in hematite ($α$-Fe$_2$O$_3$). The splitting vanishes along nodal directions and reaches maxima off the nodes, revealing the $g$-wave symmetry of the altermagnetic order parameter. These results agree with linear spin-wave theory calculations based on the altermagnetic model, which further identify the nondegenerate branches as magnons of opposite chirality and trace the splitting to symmetry-inequivalent long-range exchange interactions. Our results provide the first quantitative determination of the momentum-space symmetry of altermagnetic chiral magnons. These findings, together with hematite's high magnetic ordering temperature and low magnon damping, establish it as a promising platform for low-dissipation, symmetry-selective magnonic applications.

</details>


### [186] [Examining Convergence of Cluster Perturbation Theory and Cluster Coupled Cluster for J1-J2 Heisenberg Spin Lattices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08768)
*Jeffrey Keyes,Carlos Jimenez-Hoyos*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper extends previous cluster mean-field (cMF) based perturbative and coupled cluster calculations for the J1-J2 Heisenberg model in the thermodynamic limit, pushing from cPT4/cCCSD to cPT7/cCCSDTQ5 to demonstrate that the energy series converges, thereby confirming cMF's central claim that strongly correlated systems become weakly correlated under this transformation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The central claim of cMF is that it transforms strongly correlated systems into weakly correlated ones, enabling weakly correlated methods like perturbation theory (PT) and coupled cluster (CC) to work effectively. To validate this claim, it is essential to demonstrate that the PT and CC series actually converge, which requires going beyond the previously computed cPT4 and cCCSD levels.

Method: The authors extend previous calculations to higher orders: cPT7 for perturbation theory and cCCSDTQ5 for coupled cluster. They also develop strategies for performing cluster-based calculations efficiently in the thermodynamic limit, which is essential for these computationally more expensive higher-order calculations.

Result: The energy computed at cPT7 and cCCSDTQ5 levels shows convergence for the J1-J2 Heisenberg model in the thermodynamic limit, providing numerical evidence supporting the validity of the cMF central claim.

Conclusion: The observed convergence of the PT and CC series at higher orders confirms that cMF indeed renders strongly correlated systems weakly correlated, making weakly correlated post-cMF methods reliable. Efficient strategies for thermodynamic limit calculations are also presented.

Abstract: In previous work, it was shown that using the cluster mean field(cMF) wavefunction as the zeroth order wavefunction for perturbation theory and coupled cluster produces a reasonable approximation for the J 1-J 2 Heisenberg model in the thermodynamic limit(TDL). However, this work was limited to cPT4 and cCCSD. The central claim of cMF is that it takes strongly correlated systems and makes them weakly correlated so that PT weakly correlated methods can be used. To confirm that this claim is true, it is important to show that the PT and CC series converge. In this paper, we extend the previous calculations to cPT7 and cCCSDTQ5 where we show that the energy is converging. We also discuss how to perform cluster based calculations efficiently in the thermodynamic limit, which is crucial for these more expensive calculations.

</details>


### [187] [Positive Temperature Coefficient of Anisotropy due to $d^6L$ Groundstate in $ε$-Fe$_2$O$_3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08927)
*Rachel Nickel,Kurt Kummer,Johan van Lierop*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper investigates the anomalous positive anisotropy temperature coefficient in ε-Fe₂O₃ (125-200 K), which occurs without an obvious spin-orbit-coupled partner. By comparing pure and Cr-doped ε-Fe₂O₃ (with weakened Fe-O hybridization), the authors identify a d⁶L ground state in the pure system versus d⁶ + d⁶L² in the Cr-doped system, revealing metal-ligand hybridization as a tunable parameter for magnetic, electronic, and orbitronic properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the microscopic origin of positive dK/dT in ε-Fe₂O₃, which is anomalous because it typically requires hybridization with strongly SOC subsystems, yet this material exhibits it without an obvious SOC partner.

Method: Comparative study of pure ε-Fe₂O₃ and Cr-doped ε-Fe₂O₃ (which has weakened Fe-O hybridization) across the phase transition from low-temperature incommensurate to high-anisotropy magnetic phases, combined with electronic structure analysis to identify ground-state configurations.

Result: A d⁶L (ligand-hole) ground state is identified in ε-Fe₂O₃, while reduced hybridization in the Cr-doped system shifts the ground state toward d⁶ + d⁶L² configurations, demonstrating that metal-ligand hybridization directly controls the electronic and magnetic character.

Conclusion: Metal-ligand hybridization serves as an effective tuning knob for magnetic, electronic, and orbitronic properties in ε-Fe₂O₃, providing a mechanism for positive dK/dT without requiring an external SOC partner.

Abstract: Positive anisotropy temperature coefficients ($dK/dT>0$) usually arise from hybridization between magnetic 3d states and strongly spin-orbit-coupled (SOC) subsystems. Yet $ε$-Fe$_2$O$_3$ shows $dK/dT>0$ (125-200~K) without an obvious SOC partner. We study pure and Cr-doped (with weakened Fe-O hybridization) $ε$-Fe$_2$O$_3$ across the transition from low temperature incommensurate to high-anisotropy magnetic phases. We identify a $d^6L$ groundstate in $ε$-Fe$_2$O$_3$ while reduced hybridization yields $d^6 + d^6L^2$ in the Cr-doped system, highlighting metal-ligand hybridization as a route to tune magnetic, electronic and orbitronic properties.

</details>


### [188] [Real-time Dyson expansion for the nonequilibrium $GW$ approximation: Correlated spectra and dependence on the reference propagator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08932)
*Erik Schroedter,Jan-Philip Joost,Michael Bonitz,Vojtech Vlcek*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: The paper formulates and implements the real-time Dyson expansion (RT-DE) for the nonequilibrium GW self-energy using reference propagators of general form, benchmarking against exact diagonalization for a driven two-band lattice model and applying it to large systems to resolve excitonic satellites and shake-up structures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Full two-time nonequilibrium GW simulations scale at least cubically with propagation time, while time-local approximations or the generalized Kadanoff-Baym ansatz typically retain only mean-field spectral character. The RT-DE recovers dynamical correlations at linear cost but was previously restricted to second-order Born with mean-field references, limiting its applicability for capturing GW-level dynamical correlations in spectra.

Method: The RT-DE is extended to the nonequilibrium GW self-energy with reference propagators of general form whose off-diagonal evolution is time local. Three reference choices are assessed: Hartree-Fock propagators, propagators with statically screened exchange with nonequilibrium screening, and a correlated propagator based on the Hartree-Fock GKBA. Benchmarks are run against exact diagonalization for a driven two-band lattice model with long-range interactions.

Result: Mean-field and statically screened references yield the most accurate spectra, including satellite structures absent at the mean-field level. The GKBA-based approach best captures scattering-induced occupation dynamics but artificially broadens and splits spectral peaks. For large systems beyond exact methods, the scheme resolves the excitonic replica of the valence band and satellites identified as exciton shake-up, along with their reshaping as excitation density increases.

Conclusion: The RT-DE with GW self-energy and appropriately chosen reference propagators provides an accurate and time-linear approach to nonequilibrium spectra with dynamical correlations, with the choice of reference propagator determining the trade-off between spectral accuracy and correct occupation dynamics.

Abstract: Electronic spectra provide direct insight into the excitations and correlations of condensed matter systems. Their description requires electron correlations beyond mean field. In equilibrium, the $GW$ approximation has become the method of choice for many materials. Extending this approximation to nonequilibrium, however, is challenging. Full two-time $GW$ simulations within the nonequilibrium Green's functions approach scale at least cubically with propagation time, whereas improved scaling schemes such as time-local (adiabatic) approximations or the generalized Kadanoff--Baym ansatz usually retain only mean-field character in the spectra. The recently introduced real-time Dyson expansion recovers dynamical correlations in the spectrum at time-linear cost, but has so far been restricted to the second-order Born approximation with mean-field reference propagators. Here we formulate and implement the RT-DE for the nonequilibrium $GW$ self-energy with reference propagators of general form whose off-diagonal evolution is time local. In particular, we assess Hartree--Fock propagators, propagators with statically screened exchange with nonequilibrium screening, and a correlated propagator based on the Hartree--Fock GKBA. Benchmarks against exact diagonalization for a driven two-band lattice model with long-range interactions show that the mean-field and statically screened references yield the most accurate spectra, including satellite structures absent at mean-field level, whereas the GKBA-based approach best captures scattering-induced occupation dynamics, but artificially broadens and splits spectral peaks. When applied to large systems that are beyond the reach of exact methods, the scheme resolves the excitonic replica of the valence band and satellites identified as exciton shake-up, as well as their reshaping with increasing excitation density.

</details>


### [189] [On spectral properties of Generalized Kadanoff--Baym Ansatz](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.09067)
*Miroslav Hopjan*

Main category: cond-mat.str-el

TL;DR: This paper investigates the hidden spectral properties of the Generalized Kadanoff–Baym Ansatz (GKBA) using the extended GKBA approach. For a Hubbard cluster, ground-state spectral functions from extended GKBA show good agreement with standard Kadanoff–Baym equations at moderate interaction strengths, but significant deviations and negative (unphysical) spectral functions emerge at large interaction strengths, revealing important limitations of GKBA in strongly interacting regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Generalized Kadanoff–Baym Ansatz with mean-field propagators is widely used for efficient non-equilibrium quantum simulations due to its linear computational scaling with propagation time. However, its time-diagonal structure obscures spectral properties within the collision integral. The goal is to recover and investigate these hidden spectral properties to assess the validity and limitations of GKBA.

Method: The authors employ the extended Generalized Kadanoff–Baym Ansatz (extended GKBA) to recover the spectral properties hidden by the time-diagonal structure of the standard GKBA. They compare ground-state spectral functions of a Hubbard cluster obtained via extended GKBA against those from full Kadanoff–Baym equations, across different interaction strength regimes.

Result: At moderate interaction strengths, spectral functions from extended GKBA agree well with standard Kadanoff–Baym results. However, at large interaction strengths, significant deviations appear, and the extended GKBA spectral function becomes negative—indicating unphysical behavior.

Conclusion: Despite the computational advantages of GKBA, it has important limitations in strongly interacting regimes. For sufficiently large interactions, GKBA may produce unphysical spectral properties, as demonstrated by the negative spectral function from the extended GKBA.

Abstract: The Generalized Kadanoff--Baym Ansatz with mean-field propagators is increasingly used to simulate non-equilibrium quantum fermionic and bosonic systems.
